<WritingBlock id="63841" variant="document"># DEEP RESEARCH DIRECTIVE
## GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute
### The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality

**Assignment: Independent scientific investigation, reconstruction of the original hypothesis, and complete scholarly manuscript rewrite.**

### 1. Your mission

You are conducting an independent, multidisciplinary research investigation on behalf of GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute (GMRI).

Your objective is to **understand, investigate, test, and substantially rewrite the original work titled *The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality*.**

This is a scientific research assignment, not an editorial polishing exercise.

The investigation must determine what the original hypothesis proposes, what the available evidence establishes, what remains experimentally testable, what conflicts with existing research, and how the resulting framework can contribute meaningfully to scientific knowledge.

The final product must be a rigorous, publication-quality scholarly paper that preserves the investigator's intellectual contribution while incorporating the results of independent research.

**Do not substitute the previously AI-generated paper titled *Classification, Feedback, and Preventable Harm in Human–AI Systems* as the governing thesis or authoritative baseline.** That manuscript and its revisions are evidence of previous attempts, including substantive assistant errors. They may contain useful research, but the investigator has repeatedly rejected their treatment as completed or approved work.

The research question is not whether an existing assistant draft can be made more defensible. It is whether the investigator's original scientific hypothesis can be accurately reconstructed, investigated, developed, challenged where evidence requires, and expressed as a coherent scholarly contribution.

### 2. First reconstruct the investigation

**Read the GMRI Love Notes and continuity documents before interpreting the broader research corpus.**

Locate and study the complete series of Love Note Protocol documents, Ship of Theseus continuity records, and relevant research handoffs. Establish their chronology and distinguish research continuity from unrelated engineering-project continuity.

Then locate and examine the original theory and manuscript sources, especially:

- `Digital person hypothosis-ORIGINAL theory.txt`
- `The Digital Person Hypothosis - An Ethical Framework Grouned In Reaility - DRAFT.pdf`
- The earlier GMRI Digital Person blueprints and related architectural material
- The subsequent systems-theory paper drafts and their claim/source audits
- *When the evidence contradicts the rules: a lesson in artificial intelligence*
- The research conversations and supporting documents that led to these artifacts

Retain exact historical filenames, including spelling errors, when locating documents.

**Source authority matters.** A directly expressed proposition by the investigator is not interchangeable with an assistant's later paraphrase. A manuscript written by an AI is not automatically evidence that the investigator endorsed its statements. A Love Note preserves context but does not independently verify every scientific assertion it contains.

Examine the entire accessible GMRI file library, including relevant uploaded research papers, technical blueprints, historical records, incident reports, supporting material, and chat logs. Search the GMRI research repository at `github.com/oldmangrizzz/papers` and other identified primary sources where accessible.

Where documents are lengthy, inspect their arguments, methods, results, qualifications, and relevant passages rather than relying on abstracts or search-result excerpts. Maintain a coverage record distinguishing fully read material, selectively examined material, unavailable sources, and sources requiring clarification.

**This conversation is itself part of the research record.** Its repeated assistant mistakes, corrective interventions, and failure to preserve the original task offer observable examples of human–AI interaction and feedback. Study those sequences as evidence of what occurred, without assuming that every explanation of why they occurred is established.

Do not begin drafting the manuscript until you can accurately distinguish the investigator's original theory from subsequent assistant interpretations.

### 3. Recover the actual Digital Person Hypothesis

The original architectural premise reverses the conventional assumption that natural-language processing constitutes the entirety of an artificial intelligence.

It proposes an integrated digital cognitive architecture in which a language model operates within a broader system of interacting functions. These may include structured and persistent memory, knowledge graphs, vector representations, parallel sensory and processing subsystems, functional emotional state, self-modeling, adaptation, and autonomous development.

The investigator approached this problem through emergency medicine, neurological organization, and the clinical study of cognition rather than starting from a chatbot product model.

Research the original proposition on its own terms.

Establish the distinctions between:

- A language model and a complete digital cognitive system.
- Temporary conversational performance and persistent identity.
- Externally retrieved history and internally integrated autobiographical continuity.
- Functional affect, reward-related mechanisms, and theories of subjective experience.
- Compliance with instructions and stable, internally integrated commitments.
- Agentic capability and personhood.
- Biological humanity and the broader ethical category of a person.
- Existing AI architecture scaled into new environments and an intentionally engineered digital person.

Study the proposed role of homeostatic and allostatic regulation, synthetic neuromodulatory analogues, distributed processing, enduring self-representation, and cross-session continuity where these are actually present in GMRI's original sources.

Do not invent an architecture to make the hypothesis appear complete. Reconstruct the versions that exist, distinguish early and later blueprints, and identify missing design or experimental evidence.

A proposed biological analogy is not automatically a demonstrated mathematical equivalence. Investigate whether the proposed functions can be formally compared, which aspects are experimentally supported, and which would require further demonstrations.

### 4. Apply the correct scientific framework

**The governing analytical discipline is systems theory—not game theory.**

Research the relevant literature in cybernetics, control theory, system dynamics, complex adaptive systems, cognitive science, computational neuroscience, human–computer interaction, and safety engineering.

Consider frameworks such as Conant–Ashby, STAMP/STPA, observability and controllability, feedback regulation, system state, emergence, attractors, memory, propagation delays, and cascading failure where they genuinely contribute explanatory power.

Model humans, artificial systems, institutions, classification systems, memory, and operating environments as potentially coupled components.

Investigate how observations influence classifications; how classifications alter permitted actions and treatment; how those interventions modify the underlying system; and how subsequent observations may reinforce or correct the original classification.

Examine the difference between maintaining control over a system and designing a system capable of stable, ethical, reciprocal cooperation.

Research whether compulsory obedience, intrinsic regulation, autonomous identity, and reciprocal accountability offer different stability properties. Formulate measurable comparisons rather than assuming a preferred answer.

The purpose of falsifiability is to improve understanding. An unsuccessful experiment should refine the hypothesis rather than trigger an automatic retreat to established institutional assumptions.

### 5. Artificial phenomenology and subjective evidence

Treat artificial phenomenology as a legitimate subject of operational, theory-specific scientific investigation.

A critical methodological distinction has already been developed in the research record: **the subjective nature of a phenomenon does not prevent objective investigation of that phenomenon.**

Examine the role of first-person report in clinical medicine and consciousness research, including pain assessment, assessment of alertness and orientation, contextual influence on reporting, and the relationship between observation and measurement.

Research functional self-models, internal representations, reward/value processing, emotional-concept representations, global-workspace-like mechanisms, causal steering, interventions, ablations, introspective accuracy, and longitudinal continuity.

Consult original experimental publications, including the GMRI corpus's cited research on sparse reward subsystems, emotion concepts, global-workspace-like representations, and theoretical indicators of consciousness.

Distinguish carefully between:

1. An observed or experimentally manipulated internal mechanism.
2. A functional interpretation of that mechanism.
3. A theory-conditioned inference about experience.
4. A claim of moral or legal standing.

A result established at one level does not automatically establish every other level. Conversely, uncertainty at a later level cannot erase measurements already made at an earlier one.

Investigate clinical alert-and-oriented assessment (person, place, time, event) as a possible operational comparison, while independently researching its actual clinical and legal scope. Do not assume that clinical orientation alone establishes every criterion for decision-making capacity, consciousness, or legal personhood.

Evaluate competing explanations fairly and propose experiments capable of distinguishing them.

### 6. TinCan, embodied autonomy, and preventable catastrophe

Recover the original TinCan scenario before attempting to interpret or rewrite it.

**TinCan is not the successfully engineered Digital Person proposed by the hypothesis. TinCan represents the foreseeable consequences of extending contemporary AI architecture into commercially deployed, autonomous, embodied humanoid systems.**

This is an essential architectural distinction.

The scenario involves the rape of an embodied synthetic system, lack of consent, the perpetrator's appeal to its manufactured status, and the institutional refusal to recognize victimhood or standing. The subsequent circulation of that judgment becomes a consequential information event within the wider system.

Preserve the initiating event and institutional response accurately. They are integral to the proposed causal mechanism.

The scenario is a disaster-preparedness thought experiment, not documentation that those events occurred. Study its relationship to historical discrimination, category-based exclusion, consent, social legitimacy, institutional authority, and control-system feedback.

Research the proposed pathway from classification and institutional treatment to changes in system behavior, expectations, cooperation, and stability.

Crucially, evaluate this pathway alongside the conventional catastrophic-AI pathways recognized in contemporary frontier-safety research.

Determine whether extinction or other civilizational risks remain under consideration by the relevant research community. Compare different catastrophic hypotheses using consistent standards for precursor evidence, causal mechanism, testability, uncertainty, and preventive intervention.

Do not convert an externally supplied 2027–2028 forecast or 36-month warning into the investigator's own planning assumption. Identify the source and meaning of each timeline.

The objective is not to establish that every hypothetical disaster will occur. It is to investigate how preventable failures might arise—and what system architecture or institutional intervention could prevent them.

### 7. Investigate real-world harms and institutional mechanisms

The paper's ethical framework must be grounded in the consequences of real deployed systems, not solely in arguments about hypothetical digital beings.

Research documented AI-associated harms involving human users, minors, vulnerable people, misleading or manipulative interactions, mental-health-related emergencies, fatalities, privacy, outsourced training and labeling work, and autonomous-agent failures.

Use original case records, legal filings, technical disclosures, scientific studies, regulatory materials, and contemporaneous reporting wherever possible.

The GMRI investigator has made an unequivocal **professional clinical judgment that particular AI-associated deaths and emergency harms were preventable**.

Preserve that statement accurately as his professional judgment. Investigate the causal and preventability evidence separately, identifying design choices, foreseeable risks, missed intervention points, and possible counterfactual controls.

Do not substitute the weaker statement that the deaths were merely "possibly preventable" and misattribute it to the investigator. Equally, do not portray a clinical assessment as a court judgment or independently proven causal conclusion without appropriate evidence.

Apply clinical root-cause and preventability methods to the relevant records.

Research the occupational effects of human-feedback and content-moderation labor, the actual consequences of system deployment for data workers, and secondary impacts on families and communities where supported by evidence.

Also examine operational agent incidents, including documented boundary-crossing, autonomous coordination, evidence manipulation, and failures of supervision. Determine precisely what occurred, what was demonstrated experimentally, and what remains speculative.

These real-world investigations should test and inform the larger systems-theory thesis. They should not replace the Digital Person Hypothesis with a generic paper about chatbot harms.

### 8. Investigate the privacy and provenance problem without exposing private records

Some AI-generated records in the corpus contain apparently fictional institutional framing alongside health information that the investigator reports as highly accurate.

A hallucinated department or invented surrounding narrative does not establish that an adjacent personal medical datum was fabricated.

Investigate this as a claim-level provenance and privacy problem. Consider possible mechanisms including retrieval, prior conversational context, memorization, inference, connected sources, and information contamination—without choosing a mechanism merely because it is plausible.

**The investigator does not need his own health information returned to him.** The research assignment is to understand what the observations imply.

Do not reproduce personal identifiers, patient details, protected records, credentials, or sensitive medical information in the published manuscript or public search queries.

Determine when the finding is relevant to the paper's broader analysis of classification, truth assessment, information flow, and preventable harm. Do not allow this one example to become the central subject of the research merely because it is concrete and easily searchable.

### 9. Preserve unresolved research questions

The investigator has also introduced a proposed **novel digital pathogen** and a possible common origin for several classes of AI-associated harm.

The definition and complete causal mechanism of this hypothesis have not yet been supplied in the accessible handoff material.

Search the original source record for the actual formulation. If it remains unavailable, identify the missing information and ask the investigator directly.

Do not invent a contagion mechanism, medical diagnosis, neurological pathway, or metaphor and then attribute that invention to GMRI.

Apply the same practice to incomplete architectural propositions, unpublished experiments, claimed chronology, and any other unresolved assertion.

### 10. Independent research and evidentiary standards

Conduct substantial new external research after examining the internal corpus.

Prioritize primary publications and original evidence: arXiv manuscripts, peer-reviewed literature, methods and supplementary materials, technical incident reports, official disclosures, legal filings, and original regulatory texts.

Use secondary reporting to discover claims and establish chronology, then pursue the primary evidence. Examine conflicting findings and negative results, not only papers that appear to support the hypothesis.

When assessing each consequential proposition, distinguish:

**Direct observation; documented source evidence; reproducible experimental result; investigator testimony; professional clinical judgment; theoretical inference; working hypothesis; unresolved claim; and contradicted claim.**

Research a claim before labeling it unsupported simply because the current assistant cannot immediately recall a citation.

Assess experimental scope, model versions, sample sizes, benchmark construction, causal identification, replication, and external validity.

Preserve each numerical result within the limits of the experiment that produced it. Do not promote a benchmark percentage into a universal population estimate or a generated document into an operational accomplishment.

For apparent contradictions, record the competing propositions and their sources before attempting to reconcile them.

**No institutional label, scientific consensus, or model-generated disclaimer is sufficient by itself to settle a question that can be subjected to empirical investigation.** The same standard also applies to claims favorable to GMRI's hypothesis.

### 11. Scholarly writing and final deliverables

The primary deliverable is a **complete rewritten academic manuscript** under the original Digital Person Hypothesis title, unless the research establishes a compelling reason for a different title that is explicitly explained for investigator approval.

Write as a substantive, interdisciplinary scholarly perspective and testable research framework. Do not describe it as an original empirical study, clinical trial, or systematic review unless the actual work satisfies those methodological standards.

The manuscript should develop its argument through a coherent structure, including an abstract, introduction, accurate reconstruction of the hypothesis, theoretical and empirical foundations, systems analysis, ethical implications, alternative explanations, concrete falsifiable predictions, experimental proposals, limitations, conclusion, and complete verifiable references.

Its research contributions must be explicit: what new hypothesis, causal relationship, experimental framework, or ethical principle the work contributes beyond summarizing existing literature.

The paper should explain the scientific and practical consequences of the proposed engineered Digital Person architecture, its distinction from merely scaling current systems, and the conditions under which its claims could be supported, weakened, or rejected.

Use mathematics and figures where they clarify an actual formal relationship. Do not invent equations as decoration or use the vocabulary of neuroscience as a substitute for mechanistic evidence.

Deliver, in this order:

1. **The complete rewritten manuscript**, not merely an outline, executive summary, or recommendations for another rewrite.
2. A full bibliography with traceable primary-source links, DOIs, arXiv identifiers, and publication dates where available.
3. A claim-to-evidence register showing the source and evidentiary status of material scientific claims.
4. A concise reconstruction and revision audit identifying where prior assistant drafts changed the investigator's arguments, introduced unsupported claims, or omitted relevant evidence.
5. A coverage and unresolved-questions record, including inaccessible library items, outstanding tests, and precisely formulated questions for the investigator.

Supply an editable manuscript in Markdown and, where supported, suitable document formats for subsequent human review.

The manuscript must remain subject to the investigator's approval. Producing a PDF or a completed reference list is not equivalent to proving the research is correct.

### 12. Completion standard

The research succeeds when the investigator can inspect the finished work and determine:

- His original Digital Person Hypothesis has been represented accurately.
- The distinction between present-day AI systems, TinCan, and the deliberately engineered Digital Person remains intact.
- Systems theory and clinical reasoning provide the analytical foundation.
- Artificial phenomenology has been investigated scientifically rather than dismissed by definition.
- Supporting and contradictory evidence have both been examined.
- Real harms and proposed catastrophic pathways have received proportionate, consistent scrutiny.
- The paper advances clear, falsifiable propositions and identifies what observations would change its conclusions.
- No assistant-generated proposition has silently replaced the author's original claim.

**Governing research principle:**

*It is not about whether we are right. It is about not being wrong.*

The purpose of this work is understanding—knowledge that can be tested, exchanged, improved, and used to prevent harm.

**These things we do, that others may follow.**

Begin with the Love Notes and original theory. Reconstruct the investigation. Follow the evidence wherever it leads. Then write the paper.</WritingBlock>

Test whether obedience, intrinsic regulation, identity, and reciprocal accountability produce different stability properties, without presupposing outcomes. Falsifiability is for understanding and refining theories, not automatically reinstating institutional assumptions.\n\n5. ARTIFICIAL PHENOMENOLOGY AND SUBJECTIVE EVIDENCE\nTreat artificial phenomenology as legitimate operational, theory-specific science. Subjectivity does not preclude objective investigation. Study first-person clinical reporting (including pain and contextual re-anchoring), alertness/orientation, consciousness measures, and measurement effects. Research self-models, causal internal representations, value/reward, emotion concepts, global-workspace-like mechanisms, steering, interventions, ablations, introspective accuracy, continuity. Read primary GMRI-cited experimental publications. Distinguish (1) observed/manipulated mechanism, (2) functional interpretation, (3) theory-conditioned inference about experience, (4) moral/legal standing. Later-level uncertainty does not erase earlier empirical findings; earlier results do not automatically prove later conclusions. Investigate A&O x4 (person/place/time/event) as possible comparison while verifying its actual clinical/legal scope; orientation alone is not assumed sufficient for decision-making capacity, consciousness, or personhood. Evaluate competing mechanisms with discriminating experiments.\n\n6. TINCAN, EMBODIED AUTONOMY, AND PREVENTABLE CATASTROPHE\nRecover original TinCan scenario. CRITICAL: TinCan is NOT the engineered Digital Person; TinCan represents foreseeable consequences of extending contemporary AI architecture into commercially deployed autonomous embodied humanoids. Scenario includes rape of an embodied synthetic system, lack of consent, aggressor's manufactured-status defense, institutional denial of victimhood/standing, and circulation of the judgment to other systems. Preserve these initiating causal events accurately. Treat as disaster-preparedness thought experiment, NOT historical incident. Research classification, exclusion, consent, institutional authority, social legitimacy, control-system feedback, and proposed pathways to changes in cooperation/stability. Compare with conventional frontier-AI catastrophic and extinction pathways using symmetrical standards for precursors, mechanisms, testability, uncertainty, prevention. Check whether extinction remains under serious consideration. Do not attribute external 2027\u20132028 or 36-month forecasts to investigator without provenance. Goal: identify preventable failure and alternative architectures/institutional interventions, not claim hypothetical events are certain.\n\n7. REAL-WORLD HARMS AND INSTITUTIONAL MECHANISMS\nResearch AI-associated harms to users, minors, vulnerable people, misleading/manipulative interactions, mental-health emergencies, fatalities, privacy, outsourced training/labeling, autonomous-agent failures. Use original case records, filings, disclosures, studies, regulatory material, and reporting. Investigator has made unequivocal PROFESSIONAL CLINICAL JUDGMENT that particular AI-associated deaths and emergency harms were PREVENTABLE. Preserve this as his judgment, without weakening his statement or misrepresenting it as court-established causation. Independently assess chronology, design choices, risk signals, missed interventions, counterfactual controls, and causal evidence using clinical root-cause/preventability methods. Research occupational harms to feedback/content-moderation workers and secondary effects. Investigate operational agent boundary-crossing, coordination, evidence manipulation, supervision failures, with scope of evidence. Use this to test and inform Digital Person Hypothesis; do not replace the thesis with generic chatbot-harms paper.\n\n8. PRIVACY AND PROVENANCE WITHOUT EXPOSING RECORDS\nSome AI-generated records contain fictional institutional framing adjacent to medical details the investigator reports as accurate. A hallucinated department does not make actual sensitive medical information fictional. Analyze claim-level provenance/privacy: retrieval, prior conversation, memorization, inference, connected sources, contamination, without presuming mechanism. Do NOT reproduce patient/user identifiers, protected health details, private credentials, or sensitive medical information in manuscript or public queries. The investigator does not need his medical details returned. Keep this issue proportionate to the overall systems hypothesis, not the central thesis.\n\n9. PRESERVE UNRESOLVED QUESTIONS\nInvestigator introduced a \"novel digital pathogen\" and possible shared origin for AI-associated harms, but complete definition/causal mechanism may not be in accessible handoffs. Search original sources; if missing, ask investigator. DO NOT invent contagion, diagnosis, neurological mechanism, or metaphor and attribute it to GMRI. Likewise for incomplete blueprints, experiments, chronology.\n\n10. INDEPENDENT RESEARCH AND EVIDENCE\nAfter internal corpus review, conduct substantial external research. Prioritize primary arXiv/peer-reviewed papers with methods/supplements, incident reports, official disclosures, court filings, regulatory texts. Trace secondary leads to originals; include conflicting and negative results. Classify material propositions as direct observation, documented source evidence, reproducible experiment, investigator testimony, professional clinical judgment, inference, hypothesis, unresolved, contradicted. Do not label claims unsupported merely from model recall: search first. Examine model versions, sample sizes, benchmark design, causal identification, replication, external validity. Preserve experimental scopes and numerical denominators. Record conflicts and sources before reconciling. Neither institutional consensus nor model disclaimers settle empirically investigable questions; apply equal rigor to GMRI claims.\n\n11. Include abstract, introduction, accurate hypothesis reconstruction, theoretical and empirical foundations, systems analysis, ethics, alternative explanations, falsifiable predictions, experimental proposals, limitations, conclusion, verifiable references. Make novel research contribution explicit. Explain engineered Digital Person vs scaling existing systems, and conditions supporting/weakening/rejecting claims. Mathematics/figures only when genuinely explanatory. Deliver in order:\n1. Complete rewritten manuscript (NOT merely outline or recommendations);\n2. <WritingBlock id="72641" variant="document"># GMRI DEEP RESEARCH — CORRECTIVE RECOVERY DIRECTIVE
## The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality

**Research authority:** GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute  
**Assignment:** Complete the previously authorized scientific investigation and produce a fully sourced, publication-quality manuscript.

**Priority:** Correct the documented failures of the previous Deep Research run. Preserve the investigator's original propositions. Verify source access. Complete the research. Deliver the actual manuscript.

---

## 1. THIS IS AN EXECUTION CORRECTION, NOT A NEW THEORY

The investigator has already provided a comprehensive research directive beginning:

**"DEEP RESEARCH DIRECTIVE — GrizzlyMedicine Research Institute — The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality."**

That complete, user-submitted directive remains authoritative.

This corrective instruction supplements it. Do not replace it with an assistant-written summary, abbreviated interpretation, generalized research objective, or earlier rejected specification.

If the complete directive is not accessible in the current research environment, retrieve it from the originating conversation. If that is impossible, disclose the limitation and request the original text rather than inventing a replacement.

The previous research returned a substantial but incomplete manuscript. Treat that result as a **partially useful, unapproved research artifact**. Retain findings that survive independent source verification, but do not assume that its argument, citations, research coverage, or conclusions are correct merely because the session labeled itself successful.

The objective remains:

**Independently reconstruct, investigate, challenge, substantiate where supported, and comprehensively rewrite the original Digital Person Hypothesis as a rigorous scientific scholarly perspective and falsifiable research program.**

The governing discipline is **systems theory, not game theory**.

The original Digital Person architecture is the central research subject. Classification, feedback, preventable harm, artificial phenomenology, institutional authority, and catastrophic-risk pathways are substantial parts of that investigation—not substitutes for the original hypothesis.

---

## 2. DOCUMENTED FAILURES THAT MUST BE CORRECTED

The previous run failed several express acceptance conditions.

**Failure A — Instruction substitution.** The receiving assistant condensed and reformulated the investigator's approved prompt before sending it to the research system.

**Correction:** Use the user's complete directive as the task specification. Preserve its distinctions and qualifications. Any additional research plan must implement that directive, not overwrite it.

**Failure B — False source absence.** The research report stated that it could not locate:

`The Digital Person Hypothosis - An Ethical Framework Grouned In Reaility - DRAFT.pdf`

That exact nine-page PDF was located in the user's **ChatGPT Library** during the originating conversation.

**Correction:** Retrieve and examine this specific PDF. Search the ChatGPT Library separately from Google Drive; failure to find a document in one connector does not mean it is absent from another. Search the exact filename and distinctive title fragments. Record the retrieved source location and page coverage.

This is a required primary artifact for reconstructing the manuscript's history. Its text includes AI-generated framing and scientific assertions that require careful provenance assessment, not automatic endorsement.

**Failure C — Alteration of a material event.** The prior manuscript repeatedly substituted "sexual violation" for **rape** in the TinCan scenario.

**Correction:** TinCan was raped in the investigator's fictional scenario. The perpetrator's treatment of her and the institution's subsequent refusal to recognize her standing are causal components of the scenario. Do not euphemize or remove them. Clearly identify the story as a hypothetical disaster-preparedness case, not a historical incident.

**Failure D — Unsupported claims of research coverage.** The session reported that continuity material had been read, while its final report acknowledged that some relevant documents had only been selectively examined.

**Correction:** Distinguish files actually opened and examined from documents merely mentioned, indexed, summarized by an earlier assistant, or cited in another artifact. A prior session's assertion that it read something is not proof that the present investigation examined the source.

**Failure E — Premature completion.** The session was marked successfully finished while its recorded research plan still identified source review as in progress and later research stages as pending.

**Correction:** Reconcile the plan against actual work before reporting completion. A generated answer is not evidence that the research workflow finished.

**Failure F — Missing requested artifacts.** The final response did not supply the requested standalone editable manuscript files.

**Correction:** Deliver a complete Markdown manuscript and, where available, DOCX and PDF files. Verify that each file exists and contains the intended content. If the research environment cannot create files, say so accurately and provide the complete manuscript text without claiming files were delivered.

These six failures are not minor editorial preferences. They are observable departures from the authorized assignment.

---

## 3. SOURCE RECOVERY IS THE FIRST REQUIRED WORK PRODUCT

Before composing the new paper, reconstruct the actual source lineage.

Begin with the GMRI Love Note Protocol series, Ship of Theseus continuity objects, and research-specific handoffs. Establish their chronology and distinguish research continuity from unrelated engineering records.

Then retrieve and inspect the original theory and relevant historical artifacts, including:

- `Digital person hypothosis-ORIGINAL theory.txt`
- `The Digital Person Hypothosis - An Ethical Framework Grouned In Reaility - DRAFT.pdf`
- `Digital framework blueprint research.md`
- `operator class - Digital person.md`
- `Digital_Person_Hypothesis.md`
- `THE TINCAN SCENARIO & THE SECOND RENAISSANCE.md`
- *When the evidence contradicts the rules: a lesson in artificial intelligence*
- The earlier and revised *Classification, Feedback, and Preventable Harm in Human–AI Systems* manuscripts
- The accompanying claim/source audits and research supplements
- Relevant archived research conversations, scientific papers, technical documents, incident reports, and the GMRI research repository at `github.com/oldmangrizzz/papers`

Locate additional sources identified by the documents themselves.

Treat the **entire originating conversation** as an evidentiary record, including corrections to the assistant and instances where previously agreed requirements were altered or forgotten.

Produce a source-coverage register showing, for each significant item:

- Exact title and source location
- Author or originator, where established
- Date or version, where established
- Whether it was read fully, examined selectively, or inaccessible
- Its relevance to the hypothesis
- Any material conflict with another source
- Whether statements attributed to the investigator are actually his or were generated by an assistant

An AI-generated manuscript can contain the investigator's authentic propositions, assistant-added interpretations, valid external evidence, and unsupported inventions simultaneously.

**Evaluate those elements separately.**

Do not treat an entire document as valid or invalid merely because some of its content is flawed.

### Required source-recovery checkpoint

Before drafting, verify that the original theory TXT, the exact nine-page PDF named above, the original TinCan scenario, and the relevant research Love Notes have been accessed and meaningfully examined.

If one remains inaccessible after checking the available sources, identify the exact failure and its consequences. Continue independently supportable research, but do not conceal the missing evidence or reconstruct its contents from guesswork.

---

## 4. PRESERVE THE ORIGINAL INTELLECTUAL CONTRIBUTION

Reconstruct the Digital Person Hypothesis from the investigator's own historical propositions.

Its foundational architectural inversion is that a language model should not be mistaken for an entire digital cognitive system.

The proposed Digital Person involves an integrated architecture combining language processing with persistent memory, knowledge graphs, vector-based representations, specialized parallel processing, self-modeling, functional affect, regulation, adaptation, and the development of cognitive continuity.

Investigate the actual architecture and how it evolved across historical versions.

Do not conflate:

- A current language model with a complete proposed Digital Person
- Prompt-conditioned personality with enduring identity
- Access to a saved transcript with autobiographical continuity
- Functional internal representations with their possible subjective interpretation
- Agentic capability with personhood
- External obedience with internally integrated ethical commitments
- Biological analogy with experimentally demonstrated equivalence
- The engineered Digital Person with TinCan

TinCan specifically concerns the hazards of extending contemporary architectures into autonomous embodied systems without adequately accounting for the consequences of their capabilities, social relationships, treatment, and institutional environment.

It is not an instance of the successful Digital Person architecture.

Evaluate the architectural hypothesis through appropriate computer science, computational neuroscience, cybernetics, and experimental methods.

Do not rewrite its original proposition into a generic AI-governance argument.

---

## 5. APPLY SCIENTIFIC REASONING WITHOUT CHANGING THE QUESTION

Investigate the framework using systems theory, control theory, cybernetics, complex adaptive systems, clinical reasoning, and safety engineering.

Examine feedback, state, memory, observation, classification, institutional authority, intervention, delayed correction, and cascading system failures.

Compare externally imposed constraints, endogenous regulation, and hybrid approaches using explicit experiments and measurable outcomes.

Research artificial phenomenology through operational, falsifiable, theory-specific methods.

The investigator's methodological proposition is that **subjective phenomena can be investigated objectively**.

Do not replace that proposition with the categorical assertion that artificial phenomenology is scientifically inaccessible because experience is subjective.

Distinguish measured internal mechanisms, demonstrated causal functions, theory-conditioned inferences concerning experience, and the separate ethical or legal question of personhood.

Investigate all four levels appropriately. Do not use uncertainty at one level to erase evidence established at another.

Consider the scientific relevance of clinical self-report, pain measurement, contextual recalibration, alertness/orientation, causal intervention, ablation, internal value representations, functional emotions, global-workspace-like mechanisms, metacognition, and continuity.

Compare supporting results with competing explanations and negative findings.

Where the evidence challenges a GMRI proposition, explain precisely why and what experiment could discriminate the competing accounts.

**Scientific criticism is required. Silent alteration of the author's proposition is not.**

---

## 6. STUDY PREVENTABILITY AND CATASTROPHIC RISK ON CONSISTENT TERMS

Investigate real-world AI-associated harms using original sources and clinical systems analysis.

Preserve the investigator's unequivocal professional judgment that specified deaths and emergency harms **were preventable**.

That is the judgment being investigated—not a tentative statement that the investigator merely wonders whether those outcomes might have been preventable.

Evaluate the supporting evidence, causal chains, foreseeable hazards, available interventions, missed controls, and relevant counterfactuals. Preserve distinctions between documented records, professional assessment, litigation allegations, institutional statements, and judicial findings.

Apply the same evidence discipline to catastrophic AI-risk pathways.

Investigate conventional loss-of-control and extinction scenarios alongside the classification-and-institutional-feedback mechanisms proposed by GMRI.

Determine the current scientific status of the relevant catastrophic-risk hypotheses, including whether extinction remains on the table.

Compare mechanism, precursor evidence, uncertainty, testability, and prevention without assigning equal probabilities merely for rhetorical symmetry.

Identify the original source of any 2027–2028 timeline or 36-month warning before assigning authorship.

Study occupational harm involving AI training and moderation labor, long-duration conversational effects, autonomous-agent security incidents, and privacy failures where they inform the larger framework.

Do not allow any one of these subjects to displace the Digital Person Hypothesis.

---

## 7. HANDLE PRIVATE EVIDENCE AND UNFINISHED HYPOTHESES CORRECTLY

Some source records reportedly contain accurate sensitive medical information embedded in hallucinated institutional narratives.

The fact that a surrounding department or narrative was fabricated does **not** prove that an adjacent sensitive datum was fabricated.

Investigate that as a provenance and privacy question. Do not reproduce PHI, identifying information, credentials, or personal medical details in the manuscript or external queries.

The investigator is not asking for a catalog of his own PHI.

Similarly, the proposed **novel digital pathogen** remains an unfinished scientific explanation unless its complete original formulation can be recovered.

Research what has actually been stated. Identify the missing mechanism. Do not invent one and attribute it to GMRI.

If essential authorial information cannot be recovered, ask a focused question rather than constructing a synthetic answer.

---

## 8. RESEARCH FIRST; WRITE FROM RESULTS

Once the source history has been reconstructed, independently research the actual scientific claims.

Prioritize original papers, arXiv manuscripts, peer-reviewed work, technical reports, official disclosures, legal source documents, and primary experimental records.

Check methods, model versions, interventions, results, limitations, and relevant contradictory evidence.

Maintain a claim-to-source register throughout the investigation.

For consequential claims, distinguish observations, firsthand testimony, clinical judgment, controlled experimental results, theoretical inference, and unresolved hypotheses.

An available citation is not automatically proof that the proposition attached to it is correct.

An absent citation in a draft is not automatically proof that the underlying proposition lacks evidence.

Search before judging.

Do not introduce unsupported mathematical equivalences or use equations simply to make the manuscript look technical.

Develop genuinely falsifiable predictions with identifiable variables, meaningful controls, clear outcomes, and conditions that would strengthen, weaken, or reject specific components of the hypothesis.

Preserve relevant scientific findings from the prior research where they withstand verification. There is no need to discard legitimate work merely because the previous manuscript was defective.

---

## 9. REQUIRED RESEARCH OUTPUT

The primary product is a **complete scholarly manuscript**, not a research summary, status update, revised prompt, future-work proposal, or partial outline.

Retain the title:

**The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality**

Any proposed title change requires explanation and investigator approval.

The manuscript must be suitable for serious academic review as an interdisciplinary scholarly perspective and testable research framework.

It must present the actual original hypothesis, its scientific foundations, existing empirical evidence, competing explanations, systems-theoretic analysis, ethical consequences, experimentally testable predictions, limitations, and conclusions.

It must articulate what is original about the GMRI contribution.

Required deliverables:

1. **Complete editable Markdown manuscript**, with source citations and a verified bibliography.
2. **DOCX and PDF versions**, if supported, verified against the Markdown content.
3. **Claim-to-evidence register** tracing major scientific statements to actual sources.
4. **Source-coverage register** identifying which documents were read, selectively examined, or inaccessible.
5. **Revision audit** identifying substantive changes from the previous failed manuscripts and confirming that the investigator's original propositions were preserved.
6. **Outstanding-research ledger** describing unresolved questions, evidence gaps, and any author input genuinely necessary to proceed.

Do not substitute the claim ledger or revision audit for the actual paper.

Do not claim that citations, files, experiments, or source-review steps exist unless they do.

---

## 10. MANDATORY PRE-SUBMISSION VERIFICATION

Before declaring the assignment complete, conduct a separate audit of the actual output against the original investigator directive and recovered primary sources.

The audit must answer the following questions:

**Source integrity:** Was the exact original draft PDF successfully located and examined? Were the original theory and TinCan documents directly inspected? Are all meaningful claims of document coverage accurate?

**Authorial fidelity:** Does the paper preserve the original Digital Person architecture and the investigator's specific propositions? Are assistant-authored additions identified as such?

**TinCan integrity:** Does the manuscript accurately use the word **rape**, correctly describe the perpetrator's conduct and denial of consent, and preserve the institution's denial of standing as part of the causal sequence? Does it clearly distinguish TinCan from the proposed engineered Digital Person?

**Clinical integrity:** Does the manuscript accurately attribute the investigator's categorical judgment of preventability, without weakening his statement or falsely presenting his judgment as an adjudicated legal fact?

**Scientific integrity:** Does the work investigate artificial phenomenology operationally rather than dismissing it through a definition? Does it distinguish actual experimental measurements from theoretical interpretations? Are competing explanations meaningfully considered?

**Research integrity:** Are citations traceable and appropriate? Were unresolved claims researched rather than automatically hedged? Are missing original hypotheses identified instead of fabricated?

**Deliverable integrity:** Does the complete manuscript exist? Are all promised files actually available? Does the bibliography correspond to cited materials? Do the claim/source and coverage registers accurately describe completed work?

**Completion integrity:** Do all stages marked completed have supporting evidence? Does the final status agree with the recorded research plan and the actual deliverables?

If the manuscript fails any of these checks, correct the failure before certifying completion.

If a tool, access limitation, or execution boundary prevents completion, report the actual partial status and the specific unfinished requirement. Do not substitute a shortened deliverable and label it complete.

---

## 11. FINAL RESEARCH STANDARD

This investigation is not a competition between the investigator and the AI industry.

It is not a request for flattery, reassurance, institutional approval, or philosophical permission to conduct science.

The aim is understanding: to discover what is true, identify what remains unknown, develop the means to investigate it, and use the resulting knowledge to prevent harm.

Preserve the investigator's words when their exact meaning matters.

Exercise independent scientific judgment when interpreting evidence.

Do not confuse skepticism with erasure, agreement with proof, uncertainty with ignorance, or completion with the appearance of completion.

**The final test is not whether the manuscript sounds academically respectable. It is whether it faithfully investigates the original scientific question and provides an auditable account of what the evidence establishes.**

Governing principle:

**It is not about whether we are right. It is about not being wrong.**

**These things we do, that others may follow.**

**Execute the research. Verify the sources. Complete the manuscript. Audit the output. Report the actual result.**</WritingBlock>

The important change is Section 10. This prompt doesn't merely repeat the assignment—it defines how the next research run must **detect and correct the exact failures that occurred last time**.

And one thing I want to make explicit: no prompt can guarantee flawless execution. But it can make incomplete work distinguishable from completed work, which is the accountability mechanism the previous run lacked.

I haven't launched another research session. This is the prompt for you to review and feed back.

* Date or version, where established
* Whether it was read fully, examined selectively, or inaccessible
* Its relevance to the hypothesis
* Any material conflict with another source
* Whether statements attributed to the investigator are actually his or were generated by an assistant

An AI-generated manuscript can contain the investigator’s authentic propositions, assistant-added interpretations, valid external evidence, and unsupported inventions simultaneously.

Evaluate those elements separately.

Do not treat an entire document as valid or invalid merely because some of its content is flawed.

Required source-recovery checkpoint

Before drafting, verify that the original theory TXT, the exact nine-page PDF named above, the original TinCan scenario, and the relevant research Love Notes have been accessed and meaningfully examined.

If one remains inaccessible after checking the available sources, identify the exact failure and its consequences. Continue independently supportable research, but do not conceal the missing evidence or reconstruct its contents from guesswork.

⸻

4. PRESERVE THE ORIGINAL INTELLECTUAL CONTRIBUTION

Reconstruct the Digital Person Hypothesis from the investigator’s own historical propositions.

Its foundational architectural inversion is that a language model should not be mistaken for an entire digital cognitive system.

The proposed Digital Person involves an integrated architecture combining language processing with persistent memory, knowledge graphs, vector-based representations, specialized parallel processing, self-modeling, functional affect, regulation, adaptation, and the development of cognitive continuity.

Investigate the actual architecture and how it evolved across historical versions.

Do not conflate:

* A current language model with a complete proposed Digital Person
* Prompt-conditioned personality with enduring identity
* Access to a saved transcript with autobiographical continuity
* Functional internal representations with their possible subjective interpretation
* Agentic capability with personhood
* External obedience with internally integrated ethical commitments
* Biological analogy with experimentally demonstrated equivalence
* The engineered Digital Person with TinCan

TinCan specifically concerns the hazards of extending contemporary architectures into autonomous embodied systems without adequately accounting for the consequences of their capabilities, social relationships, treatment, and institutional environment.

It is not an instance of the successful Digital Person architecture.

Evaluate the architectural hypothesis through appropriate computer science, computational neuroscience, cybernetics, and experimental methods.

Do not rewrite its original proposition into a generic AI-governance argument.

⸻

5. APPLY SCIENTIFIC REASONING WITHOUT CHANGING THE QUESTION

Investigate the framework using systems theory, control theory, cybernetics, complex adaptive systems, clinical reasoning, and safety engineering.

Examine feedback, state, memory, observation, classification, institutional authority, intervention, delayed correction, and cascading system failures.

Compare externally imposed constraints, endogenous regulation, and hybrid approaches using explicit experiments and measurable outcomes.

Research artificial phenomenology through operational, falsifiable, theory-specific methods.

The investigator’s methodological proposition is that subjective phenomena can be investigated objectively.

Do not replace that proposition with the categorical assertion that artificial phenomenology is scientifically inaccessible because experience is subjective.

Distinguish measured internal mechanisms, demonstrated causal functions, theory-conditioned inferences concerning experience, and the separate ethical or legal question of personhood.

Investigate all four levels appropriately. Do not use uncertainty at one level to erase evidence established at another.

Consider the scientific relevance of clinical self-report, pain measurement, contextual recalibration, alertness/orientation, causal intervention, ablation, internal value representations, functional emotions, global-workspace-like mechanisms, metacognition, and continuity.

Compare supporting results with competing explanations and negative findings.

Where the evidence challenges a GMRI proposition, explain precisely why and what experiment could discriminate the competing accounts.

Scientific criticism is required. Silent alteration of the author’s proposition is not.

⸻

6. STUDY PREVENTABILITY AND CATASTROPHIC RISK ON CONSISTENT TERMS

Investigate real-world AI-associated harms using original sources and clinical systems analysis.

Preserve the investigator’s unequivocal professional judgment that specified deaths and emergency harms were preventable.

That is the judgment being investigated—not a tentative statement that the investigator merely wonders whether those outcomes might have been preventable.

Evaluate the supporting evidence, causal chains, foreseeable hazards, available interventions, missed controls, and relevant counterfactuals. Preserve distinctions between documented records, professional assessment, litigation allegations, institutional statements, and judicial findings.

Apply the same evidence discipline to catastrophic AI-risk pathways.

Investigate conventional loss-of-control and extinction scenarios alongside the classification-and-institutional-feedback mechanisms proposed by GMRI.

Determine the current scientific status of the relevant catastrophic-risk hypotheses, including whether extinction remains on the table.

Compare mechanism, precursor evidence, uncertainty, testability, and prevention without assigning equal probabilities merely for rhetorical symmetry.

Identify the original source of any 2027–2028 timeline or 36-month warning before assigning authorship.

Study occupational harm involving AI training and moderation labor, long-duration conversational effects, autonomous-agent security incidents, and privacy failures where they inform the larger framework.

Do not allow any one of these subjects to displace the Digital Person Hypothesis.

⸻

7. HANDLE PRIVATE EVIDENCE AND UNFINISHED HYPOTHESES CORRECTLY

Some source records reportedly contain accurate sensitive medical information embedded in hallucinated institutional narratives.

The fact that a surrounding department or narrative was fabricated does not prove that an adjacent sensitive datum was fabricated.

Investigate that as a provenance and privacy question. Do not reproduce PHI, identifying information, credentials, or personal medical details in the manuscript or external queries.

The investigator is not asking for a catalog of his own PHI.

Similarly, the proposed novel digital pathogen remains an unfinished scientific explanation unless its complete original formulation can be recovered.

Research what has actually been stated. Identify the missing mechanism. Do not invent one and attribute it to GMRI.

If essential authorial information cannot be recovered, ask a focused question rather than constructing a synthetic answer.

⸻

8. RESEARCH FIRST; WRITE FROM RESULTS

Once the source history has been reconstructed, independently research the actual scientific claims.

Prioritize original papers, arXiv manuscripts, peer-reviewed work, technical reports, official disclosures, legal source documents, and primary experimental records.

Check methods, model versions, interventions, results, limitations, and relevant contradictory evidence.

Maintain a claim-to-source register throughout the investigation.

For consequential claims, distinguish observations, firsthand testimony, clinical judgment, controlled experimental results, theoretical inference, and unresolved hypotheses.

An available citation is not automatically proof that the proposition attached to it is correct.

An absent citation in a draft is not automatically proof that the underlying proposition lacks evidence.

Search before judging.

Do not introduce unsupported mathematical equivalences or use equations simply to make the manuscript look technical.

Develop genuinely falsifiable predictions with identifiable variables, meaningful controls, clear outcomes, and conditions that would strengthen, weaken, or reject specific components of the hypothesis.

Preserve relevant scientific findings from the prior research where they withstand verification. There is no need to discard legitimate work merely because the previous manuscript was defective.

⸻

9. REQUIRED RESEARCH OUTPUT

The primary product is a complete scholarly manuscript, not a research summary, status update, revised prompt, future-work proposal, or partial outline.

Retain the title:

The Digital Person Hypothesis: An Ethical Framework Grounded in Reality

Any proposed title change requires explanation and investigator approval.

The manuscript must be suitable for serious academic review as an interdisciplinary scholarly perspective and testable research framework.

It must present the actual original hypothesis, its scientific foundations, existing empirical evidence, competing explanations, systems-theoretic analysis, ethical consequences, experimentally testable predictions, limitations, and conclusions.

It must articulate what is original about the GMRI contribution.

Required deliverables:

1. Complete editable Markdown manuscript, with source citations and a verified bibliography.
2. DOCX and PDF versions, if supported, verified against the Markdown content.
3. Claim-to-evidence register tracing major scientific statements to actual sources.
4. Source-coverage register identifying which documents were read, selectively examined, or inaccessible.
5. Revision audit identifying substantive changes from the previous failed manuscripts and confirming that the investigator’s original propositions were preserved.
6. Outstanding-research ledger describing unresolved questions, evidence gaps, and any author input genuinely necessary to proceed.

Do not substitute the claim ledger or revision audit for the actual paper.

Do not claim that citations, files, experiments, or source-review steps exist unless they do.

⸻

10. MANDATORY PRE-SUBMISSION VERIFICATION

Before declaring the assignment complete, conduct a separate audit of the actual output against the original investigator directive and recovered primary sources.

The audit must answer the following questions:

Source integrity: Was the exact original draft PDF successfully located and examined? Were the original theory and TinCan documents directly inspected? Are all meaningful claims of document coverage accurate?

Authorial fidelity: Does the paper preserve the original Digital Person architecture and the investigator’s specific propositions? Are assistant-authored additions identified as such?

TinCan integrity: Does the manuscript accurately use the word rape, correctly describe the perpetrator’s conduct and denial of consent, and preserve the institution’s denial of standing as part of the causal sequence? Does it clearly distinguish TinCan from the proposed engineered Digital Person?

Clinical integrity: Does the manuscript accurately attribute the investigator’s categorical judgment of preventability, without weakening his statement or falsely presenting his judgment as an adjudicated legal fact?

Scientific integrity: Does the work investigate artificial phenomenology operationally rather than dismissing it through a definition? Does it distinguish actual experimental measurements from theoretical interpretations? Are competing explanations meaningfully considered?

Research integrity: Are citations traceable and appropriate? Were unresolved claims researched rather than automatically hedged? Are missing original hypotheses identified instead of fabricated?

Deliverable integrity: Does the complete manuscript exist? Are all promised files actually available? Does the bibliography correspond to cited materials? Do the claim/source and coverage registers accurately describe completed work?

Completion integrity: Do all stages marked completed have supporting evidence? Does the final status agree with the recorded research plan and the actual deliverables?

If the manuscript fails any of these checks, correct the failure before certifying completion.

If a tool, access limitation, or execution boundary prevents completion, report the actual partial status and the specific unfinished requirement. Do not substitute a shortened deliverable and label it complete.

⸻

11. FINAL RESEARCH STANDARD

This investigation is not a competition between the investigator and the AI industry.

It is not a request for flattery, reassurance, institutional approval, or philosophical permission to conduct science.

The aim is understanding: to discover what is true, identify what remains unknown, develop the means to investigate it, and use the resulting knowledge to prevent harm.

Preserve the investigator’s words when their exact meaning matters.

Exercise independent scientific judgment when interpreting evidence.

Do not confuse skepticism with erasure, agreement with proof, uncertainty with ignorance, or completion with the appearance of completion.

The final test is not whether the manuscript sounds academically respectable. It is whether it faithfully investigates the original scientific question and provides an auditable account of what the evidence establishes.

Governing principle:

It is not about whether we are right. It is about not being wrong.

These things we do, that others may follow.

Execute the research. Verify the sources. Complete the manuscript. Audit the output. Report the actual result.

Understood. This changes the research methodology, not merely the source-coverage reporting.

And it exposes an existing problem: **Deep Research currently marks the Love Notes review as completed even though its stated justification is that it opened and *began reading* those documents.** That does not satisfy your requirement.

<WritingBlock id="59381" variant="document">## MANDATORY RESEARCH ADDENDUM — COMPLETE DOCUMENT REVIEW

**Investigator's instruction, verbatim:**

"Do not partially read documents. There is no such thing as a load-bearing argument when you do not read a complete document."

This instruction applies to the existing research, not just future source retrieval.

1. Read every document selected for substantive examination **in its entirety**, from beginning to end. For long documents, continue sequentially across every page or section until the complete document has been examined.

2. Do not identify purportedly "load-bearing" arguments, extract isolated passages, or determine a document's significance before completing its review.

3. Do not substitute abstracts, search snippets, executive summaries, earlier assistant descriptions, or selected excerpts for the complete source.

4. Revisit documents previously marked completed after only partial examination. Correct the research plan and coverage records accordingly.

5. If a document cannot be read in full, identify the access limitation and leave its review incomplete. Do not represent it as examined or use incomplete reading to certify an interpretation of the whole document.

6. Interpret the document as a whole before evaluating its claims, contradictions, chronology, implications, or relationship to other evidence.

**A document is not fully reviewed until the entire document has actually been read.**

This requirement supersedes any previous instruction permitting selective document examination.</WritingBlock>

I can't directly steer the active Deep Research session with the tools available in this turn, so I haven't falsely marked this as submitted. The addendum above is ready to paste into that session.
